## Port Authorities Operations Regulations {#port-authorities-operations-regulations .Title-of-Act}

[SOR]{.abbr title="Statutory Orders and Regulations"}/2000-55
:::

[CANADA MARINE ACT](/eng/acts/C-6.7)

Registration 2000-02-10

Port Authorities Operations Regulations

P.C. 2000-134 2000-02-10
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
Her Excellency the Governor General in Council, on the recommendation of
the Minister of Transport, pursuant to subsection 62(1) of the [Canada
Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7)[[Footnote
]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#footnotea){#footnotea-ID0EDBAA}, hereby makes the
annexed [Port Authorities Operations
Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2000-55).

- ::: Footnote
  [[Return to footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^a^](#footnotea-ID0EDBAA){#footnotea}S.C. 1998, c. 10
  :::
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-663971 .Part}

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in these Regulations.

[[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Canada Marine
    Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7). ([Loi]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[activity list]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[activity list]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of a port, means
    the Part of Schedule 1 particular to that port. ([liste des
    activités]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[dangerous goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[dangerous goods]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned in
    section 2 of the [Transportation of Dangerous Goods Act,
    1992](/eng/acts/T-19.01). ([marchandises
    dangereuses]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[designated area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[designated area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area designated by
    a port authority in respect of an activity set out in column 1 of an
    activity list. ([endroit désigné]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a fee fixed under subsection 49(1)
    of the [Canada Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7) or accepted by a port
    authority under section 53 of that Act. ([droit]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[hot work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hot work]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any work that uses flame or
    that can produce a source of ignition, such as heating, cutting or
    welding. ([travail à chaud]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-663979 .Part}

### [Where Regulations Apply]{.HTitleText2} {#h-663980 .Subheading}

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 3, these
Regulations apply in respect of the navigable waters of a port, works
and activities in a port and the property managed, held or occupied by a
port authority.

### [Where Regulations Do Not Apply --- [Canadian Navigable Waters Act](/eng/acts/N-22)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-663982 .Subheading}

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations do not
apply in respect of that aspect of a work, within the meaning of the
[Canadian Navigable Waters Act](/eng/acts/N-22), that involves a
determination under that Act by the Minister as to whether the work will
interfere with navigation if the work is built, placed, rebuilt,
repaired or altered in, on, over, under, through or across the navigable
waters of a port by

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a port authority other than a port authority set out
  in Schedule 2; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person other than a person conducting those
  activities on behalf of a port authority set out in Schedule 2.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 1(F)
- [2019, c. 28, s. 187](#1158429-1199368){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1158429-1199368.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-3-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 3"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Binding on Her Majesty]{.HTitleText1} {#h-663988 .Part}

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** These Regulations are
binding on Her Majesty in right of Canada or a province.

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Safety and Order in Ports]{.HTitleText1} {#h-663990 .Part}

### [Prohibitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-663991 .Subheading}

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise authorized
under these Regulations, no person shall, by act or omission, do
anything or permit anything to be done in a port that has or is likely
to have any of the following results:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to jeopardize the safety or health of persons in the
  port;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to interfere with navigation;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} to obstruct or threaten any part of the port;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} to interfere with an authorized activity in the port;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} to divert the flow of a river or stream, cause or
  affect currents, cause silting or the accumulation of material or
  otherwise reduce the depth of the waters of the port;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} to cause a nuisance;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} to cause damage to ships or other property;

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} to adversely affect soil, air or water quality; or

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} to adversely affect port operations or the property
  managed, held or occupied by the port authority.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 2(F)
:::

**[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** No person shall, in a port,
conduct an activity set out in column 1 of the activity list if an "X"
is set out in column 4.

### [Access to Port Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664005 .Subheading}

**[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** No person shall access any
area managed, held or occupied by a port authority unless

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person accesses the area to conduct legitimate
  business in the port;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person is authorized by the port authority to
  access the area; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} access is not restricted by a sign, a device or in
  some other way such as by a fence.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 3
:::

### [Signs]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664012 .Subheading}

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** A port authority may have
signs posted or devices installed for the purpose of ensuring the safety
of persons and property in a port, the environmental protection of the
port or the management of the marine infrastructure and services of the
port in a commercial manner.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 4
:::

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person in a port must comply with the instructions on signs posted and
  with devices installed under the authority of the port authority
  unless the person is authorized by the port authority to do otherwise.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall remove, mark or deface any sign or
  device in a port.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 5
:::

### [Operation of Vehicles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664021 .Subheading}

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person who operates a vehicle on property managed, held or occupied by
  a port authority shall do so in a safe manner and at a speed that does
  not exceed the lower of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the speed limit posted under the authority of the
    port authority on signs on the property, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a lower speed that is warranted by prevailing
    weather conditions or by the movement or storage of equipment,
    trains or goods.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2002-179, s. 1\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 1
:::

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A port
  authority may have signs and devices installed on property managed,
  held or occupied by it respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the safe operation of vehicles; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the parking or stopping of vehicles, including
    signs and devices restricting or prohibiting parking or stopping.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who operates a vehicle on property
  managed, held or occupied by a port authority shall obey

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the instructions on any sign posted or device
    placed under the authority of the port authority and applicable to
    the person, vehicle or property; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} traffic directions given by a person authorized for
    that purpose by the port authority.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The owner of a vehicle that is parked or stopped
  contrary to an instruction or direction referred to in subsection (2)
  and any person in possession of the vehicle may be held liable.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 2
- SOR/2004-255, s. 6
:::

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** A port authority may have
a vehicle moved or stored at the time at which the vehicle is found to
be parked or stopped contrary to this section if the vehicle is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} apparently abandoned;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} parked or stopped

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in a place or manner that creates a hazard or
    obstruction, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at a time that is not within the posted parking or
    stopping hours; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} parked or stopped in an area

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} not posted as a parking or stopping area, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} posted as a no-parking or no-stopping area.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 3
- SOR/2004-255, s. 7
:::

### [Removal --- Property or Waters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664049 .Subheading}

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Unless
  otherwise authorized under these Regulations, a person who, in a port,
  drops, deposits, discharges or spills refuse, a substance that
  pollutes, cargo, ship's gear or any other thing that interferes with
  navigation shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} immediately make every technically and economically
    feasible effort to remove it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} notify the port authority of the incident without
    delay and provide a description of what was dropped, deposited,
    discharged or spilled and its approximate location.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the person does not remove the refuse, substance,
  cargo, ship's gear or thing immediately, the port authority may
  conduct the removal and, if the thing removed interfered with
  navigation, the removal may be done at the expense of the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 8
- SOR/2005-326, s. 1(F)
:::

### [Fire Protection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664057 .Subheading}

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** Every person in a port
shall follow the fire protection and prevention measures reasonably
necessary for the safety of persons and property in the port, taking
into account the activities and goods in the port.

### [Dangerous Situations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664059 .Subheading}

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** Every person who by act
or omission causes a dangerous situation in a port shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} take one of the following precautions:

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} post the notices, deploy the lights and erect the
    fences, barricades or other devices that are necessary to prevent
    accidents and to protect persons and property, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} station a person at the site of the dangerous
    situation to warn persons of the danger;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} take appropriate measures to prevent injury to
  persons or damage to property; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} notify the port authority without delay as to the
  nature of the dangerous situation and the precautions that have been
  taken and their location.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 9
:::

### [Emergencies]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664068 .Subheading}

**[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** Despite any other
provision of these Regulations, a person may, in a port, conduct an
activity set out in column 1 of the activity list without having a
contract, lease or licence or an authorization from the port authority
or complying with the conditions of an authorization for the duration of
an emergency situation if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the activity is required as a result of an emergency
  situation that jeopardizes the safety of persons or threatens to cause
  damage to property or the environment; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} without delay, the person conducting the activity
  submits a report to the port authority describing the activity and
  explaining why the situation was regarded as an emergency.

**[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** If a situation causes or
is likely to cause death, bodily injury or any other emergency situation
in a port damages or is likely to damage property or the environment,
every person directly involved in the situation and, in the case of an
activity conducted under a contract, lease, licence or authorization,
the person authorized to conduct the activity shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} without delay, notify the port authority that there
  is an emergency situation;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} submit a detailed report of the emergency situation
  to the port authority as soon as possible after it begins; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} at the request of the port authority, submit with the
  report to the port authority a copy of each report of the emergency
  situation that the person makes to municipal, provincial and federal
  authorities.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 4(F)
:::

### [Accidents and Incidents]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664078 .Subheading}

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** A person who does
anything in a port that results in an incident involving material loss
or damage or an explosion, fire, accident, grounding, stranding or
incident of pollution shall submit a detailed report of the incident to
the port authority without delay.

### [Precautionary Measures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664080 .Subheading}

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, in
  a port, a person conducts an activity, other than an activity set out
  in Schedule 1, that is likely to have any of the results prohibited
  under section 5, a port authority may instruct the person conducting
  the activity to cease the activity or to take the precautions
  reasonably necessary to mitigate or prevent the result.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person shall immediately comply with the
  instructions of the port authority.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 10
:::

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Port Authority Activities]{.HTitleText1} {#h-664086 .Part}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664087 .Subheading}

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** If a port authority is
the proponent of an activity that is set out in column 1 of the activity
list and is likely to have any of the results prohibited under section
5, it shall take appropriate measures designed to mitigate or prevent
the result, if technically and economically feasible, taking into
account the responsibility of the port authority for the safety of
persons and property in the port, the environmental protection of the
port and the management of the marine infrastructure and services of the
port in a commercial manner.

### [Works within the Meaning of the [Canadian Navigable Waters Act](/eng/acts/N-22)]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664089 .Subheading}

#### [Authority to Build]{.HTitleText3} {#h-664090 .Subheading}

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** Subject to its letters
patent and section 22, a port authority set out in Schedule 2 or a
person acting on behalf of a port authority set out in Schedule 2 may
build, place, rebuild, repair or alter in, on, over, under, through or
across the navigable waters of the port a work within the meaning of the
[Canadian Navigable Waters Act](/eng/acts/N-22) that may interfere with
navigation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 11(F)
- [2019, c. 28, s. 187](#1158429-1199365){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1158429-1199365.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Assessments and Reports]{.HTitleText3} {#h-664094 .Subheading}

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before
  carrying out an activity referred to in section 21, the port authority
  shall conduct an assessment of the impact of the work on navigation in
  the port.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The port authority shall ensure that a report that
  summarizes the assessment is prepared before the activity is begun in
  respect of the work.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The port authority shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the assessment indicates that the work would
    have an adverse effect on safety in respect of navigation in the
    port, take appropriate measures designed to mitigate the effect, if
    technically and economically feasible; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the assessment indicates that the work would
    interfere with any other aspect of navigation, take measures to
    ensure that the work is consistent with the objective declared in
    section 4 of the [Canada Marine Act](/eng/acts/C-6.7).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2004-255, s. 12(F)
:::

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Authorizations and Instructions for Activities in Ports]{.HTitleText1} {#h-664103 .Part}

### [Activities under Contracts, Leases and Licences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664104 .Subheading}

**[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** A person may, in a port,
conduct an activity set out in column 1 of the activity list if
authorized to do so in writing expressly or by necessary implication
under a contract or lease entered into with, or a licence granted by,
the port authority.

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** If, by entering into a
contract or lease or granting a licence, a port authority authorizes an
activity set out in column 1 of the activity list that has or is likely
to have any of the results prohibited under section 5, the port
authority shall stipulate as a condition of the contract, lease or
licence that the person with whom the contract or lease is made or to
whom the licence is granted shall take measures designed to mitigate or
prevent the result, if technically and economically feasible.

### [Authorizations by Posted Signs or Forms]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664107 .Subheading}

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  a port authority may, by means of posted signs or by forms, give an
  authorization under this section to conduct in the port an activity
  set out in column 1 of the activity list if an "X" is set out in
  column 2.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the conduct of the activity is not likely to have
  a result prohibited under section 5, the port authority may give the
  authorization without conditions, as long as the authorization is
  posted in a prominent place clearly visible to persons wanting to
  conduct the activity.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the conduct of the activity is likely to have a
  result prohibited under section 5, the port authority may give the
  authorization only if, for the activity, it

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishes conditions designed to mitigate or
    prevent the result; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} communicates the conditions by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} posting them in a prominent place clearly visible
      to persons wanting to conduct the activity, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} setting them out in forms that are readily
      available to persons wanting to conduct the activity.

<!-- -->

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall conduct an activity set out in column 1 of the activity
  list if an "X" is set out in column 2 unless the person complies with
  the conditions, if any, for conducting the activity that are posted or
  set out on forms and pays the applicable fee, if any.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a condition for conducting the activity is to
  complete a checklist, the person conducting the activity shall keep
  the checklist readily available for inspection.

### [Authorizations to Persons]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664119 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2002-179, s. 5

\]
:::

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A port
  authority may give a written authorization under this section to a
  person to conduct, in the port, an activity set out in column 1 of the
  activity list if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an "X" is set out in column 3; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an "X" is set out in column 2 and the person or any
    person who would be covered by the authorization is unable to comply
    with the conditions posted or set out on forms for the conduct of
    the activity under section 25.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of a request for an authorization, along
  with payment of the applicable fee, if any, and the information
  required under subsection 28(2), the port authority shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give its authorization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the results of the conduct of the activity are
    uncertain or if the conduct of the activity is likely to have any of
    the results prohibited under section 5,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} refuse to give its authorization, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} give its authorization subject to conditions
      designed to mitigate or prevent the results; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the port authority required that the person
    obtain insurance coverage, performance security or damage security
    in respect of the conduct of the activity and none is obtained or
    that which is obtained is inadequate, refuse to give its
    authorization.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2000-140, s. 1(E)
- SOR/2002-179, s. 6
- SOR/2004-255, s. 13(F)
- SOR/2013-42, s. 1(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-27-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 27"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, in a port, conduct an activity set out in column 1 of
  the activity list if an "X" is set out in column 3 unless the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} obtains an authorization under section 27 or is
    covered by an authorization given under that section; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} complies with the conditions, if any, of the
    authorization.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person that seeks an authorization from a port
  authority to conduct an activity in the port shall provide to the port
  authority

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name and address of the person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicable fee, if any;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} information relevant to the proposed activity and
    required by the port authority to assess the likelihood of the
    occurrence of any of the results prohibited under section 5;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if required by the port authority, proof that the
    applicant has an insurance policy that provides adequate coverage
    for the activity, names the port authority as an additional insured
    and provides for the insurer to notify the port authority in the
    event that the policy is amended or cancelled; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if required by the port authority, performance
    security and damage security in respect of the conduct of the
    activity.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2000-140, s. 2
- SOR/2002-179, s. 7
- SOR/2004-255, s. 14
:::

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** A port authority may
cancel an authorization given under section 27 or change the conditions
to which an authorization is subject if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of the activity has a result prohibited
  under section 5 or, as a result of a change in circumstances, becomes
  likely to have such a result;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the insurance coverage, performance security or
  damage security obtained by the person in respect of the conduct of
  the activity becomes inadequate for the activity or is cancelled;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the authorization was based on incorrect or
  misleading information; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the person to whom the authorization is given or any
  person covered by the authorization does not comply with a condition
  of the authorization.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 8
:::

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If an
  authorization given under section 27 is cancelled, the port authority
  shall give notice of the cancellation to the person to whom the
  authorization was given.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The cancellation is effective at the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the end of the fifth day after the notice of
    cancellation is sent by registered mail to the address provided in
    the application for the authorization,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two hours after a facsimile or electronic
    transmission of the notice of cancellation is sent to the address
    provided in the application for the authorization, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} immediately on service of the notice of
    cancellation at the address provided in the application for the
    authorization.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 9
:::

### [Instructions to Cease, Remove, Return and Restore]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664161 .Subheading}

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A port
  authority may instruct a person to take any of the actions prescribed
  under subsection (2) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person conducts an activity that is prohibited
    under section 6;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person conducts an activity for which an
    authorization is required under section 27 without first obtaining
    the authorization or without being covered by one;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person or any person covered by an
    authorization fails to comply with a condition of the authorization;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the authorization to conduct the activity is
    cancelled under section 29; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an activity for which no
    authorization is required under these Regulations, the conduct of
    the activity has a result prohibited under section 5.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The actions are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to cease the activity or comply with the conditions
    for conducting the activity; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person is instructed to cease the activity,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to remove anything brought into the port in
      connection with the activity,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to return to the port anything that was removed
      from it in connection with the activity, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to restore the property affected by the
      activity to its former state.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person shall immediately comply with the
  instructions of the port authority.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the person fails to remove the thing or to restore
  the property immediately, the port authority may conduct the removal
  or restoration and may store the thing.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the thing removed or stored by the port authority
  interfered with navigation, the removal and, if applicable, the
  storage may be done at the expense of the person.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2002-179, s. 10
- SOR/2004-255, s. 15
- SOR/2013-42, s. 2(F)
:::

### [Container Trucking --- Port Metro Vancouver]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664180 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2014-86, s. 1
- SOR/2014-308, s. 1

\]
:::

- **[[31.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in
  this section.

  [[Authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Authority]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Vancouver Fraser Port
      Authority. ([Administration]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[authorization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[authorization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a written
      authorization issued by the Authority to transport containers by
      truck in Port Metro Vancouver. ([autorisation]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Container Trucking Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Container Trucking Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the British
      Columbia Container Trucking Act, S.B.C. 2014, c. 28. ([loi sur le
      transport de conteneurs par camion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Port Metro Vancouver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Port Metro Vancouver]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Authority's
      port. ([Port Metro Vancouver]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) and (6), the Authority
  shall not permit a truck to gain access to Port Metro Vancouver for
  the purpose of transporting a container unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the driver of the truck is employed by or is
    acting, either directly or indirectly, on behalf of a person who
    holds a valid authorization;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person who holds the authorization also holds a
    licence under the Container Trucking Act to transport containers by
    truck in Port Metro Vancouver; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the licence is not under suspension.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An authorization is valid if the holder complies
  with, or ensures that the holder's drivers comply with, any of the
  following requirements that are established by the Authority and
  specified in the authorization:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} requirements respecting an appointment or
    reservation system for trucks;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requirements respecting the identification of
    trucks that gain access to Port Metro Vancouver; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} requirements respecting the tracking and monitoring
    of trucks in Port Metro Vancouver.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a licence referred to in
  paragraph (2)(b) includes an authorization that is deemed to be a
  licence under the Container Trucking Act.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply if there is an
  emergency that poses a risk to the safety of persons or property in
  Port Metro Vancouver or a risk to the environment and if containers
  must be transported by truck to mitigate or help mitigate that risk.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs (2)(b) and (c) do not apply in respect of
  a truck that is transporting a container to or from a location outside
  of the Lower Mainland of British Columbia.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2006-278, s. 1
- SOR/2007-171, s. 1
- SOR/2014-86, s. 2
- SOR/2014-308, s. 1
:::

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Ships and Cargoes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-664202 .Part}

### [Information in Respect of Ships and Cargoes]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664203 .Subheading}

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies in respect of ships of a class for which fees are
  fixed under subsection 49(1) of the Act.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of pleasure
  craft.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} At least 24 hours before a ship enters a port, the
  owner or the person in charge of the ship shall provide the following
  information to the port authority if the information has not already
  been provided for the purpose of obtaining a traffic clearance:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the name of the ship, its port of registry and its
    identification number as it appears in the Lloyd's Register of
    Shipping or on the ship's certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the name of the owner, the master and the agent of
    the ship;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the gross tonnage of the ship and its length
    overall;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the port from which and the date on which the ship
    began its voyage;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the estimated time of arrival of the ship in the
    port;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the estimated draught of the ship on arrival in the
    port and on departure;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the description, quantity and tonnage of dangerous
    goods that are in transit on the ship or that will be loaded,
    unloaded or transferred in the port, in the form provided by the
    port authority;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the description, quantity and tonnage of goods to
    be loaded, unloaded or transferred in the port;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the number of passengers in transit on the ship,
    embarking on the ship or disembarking from the ship;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} if the ship is not engaged in cargo or passenger
    operations, the purpose of the ship's visit to the port;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the last port of call of the ship and its next
    intended port of call; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} any other information requested by the port
    authority and relevant to assessing the likelihood of the occurrence
    of any of the results prohibited under section 5 or to the
    management of the marine infrastructure and services of the port in
    a commercial manner.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The owner or the person in charge of the ship shall
  provide the port authority with the description, quantity and tonnage
  of the cargo loaded, unloaded or transferred at each berth or
  anchorage within 24 hours after the loading, unloading or transferral.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The information shall be provided by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} delivering it by hand in the form of a certificate
    signed by the owner or person in charge of the ship; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sending it by facsimile or electronic transmission.

### [Cargo Operations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664224 .Subheading}

- **[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  ship is waiting for another ship to load, unload or transfer cargo to
  get a berth or anchorage, the owner or the person in charge of the
  other ship shall ensure that, regardless of whether working around the
  clock is necessary or overtime charges are incurred,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the loading, unloading or transfer operations are
    conducted with dispatch; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the cargo is moved with dispatch from the immediate
    vicinity of the berth or anchorage to enable the waiting ship to
    load, unload or transfer its cargo.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the loading, unloading or transfer operations of
  the ship, or the movement of its cargo, are not conducted with
  dispatch, the port authority may instruct the owner or the person in
  charge of the ship to move

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ship from the berth or anchorage to allow the
    waiting ship to berth or to anchor and begin its loading, unloading
    or transfer operations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the cargo from the immediate vicinity of the berth
    or anchorage.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, to allow a waiting ship to berth or to anchor
  promptly, the loading, unloading or transfer operations of a ship, or
  the movement of its cargo, are conducted around the clock or the ship
  or cargo is moved on the instructions of the port authority, the owner
  or person in charge of the waiting ship shall ensure that, regardless
  of whether working around the clock is necessary or overtime charges
  are incurred,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the loading, unloading or transfer operations of
    the waiting ship are conducted with dispatch; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the cargo of the waiting ship is moved with
    dispatch from the immediate vicinity of the berth or anchorage.

### [Crewing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664235 .Subheading}

**[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** The owner or the person
in charge of a ship berthed, moored or anchored in a port shall ensure
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the harbour master is kept informed of the location
  and telephone number of a person who can take command of the ship
  immediately at the request of the harbour master if it is necessary
  for the safety of persons or property in the port to move the ship;
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the ship is kept in a state of readiness so that it
  can be moved quickly.

### [Arrangements for Towing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-664239 .Subheading}

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** The owner or the person
in charge of a ship that is berthed, moored or anchored in a port shall
ensure that the ship is fitted with arrangements suitable for attaching
a towing line so that the ship can be towed from its berth, mooring or
anchorage if moving the ship is necessary for the safety of persons or
property in the port.

headers="t2652h1 t2652h6 t2652h91">(a) in a designated area under a
